SAM Volume II | Reading this edition

Brady | 19 October 2026

Reading this edition
This edition develops a dedicated ﬁve-chapter spin section within the complete matter manuscript. The spin account begins
with exact joint state counting and ﬁnite thermal structure, follows a speciﬁed microscopic source through hidden memory and
ordered readings, develops composition and kinetic response, and brings the physical receiver through the latest completed source
Dirichlet result. A synthesis chapter connects these results to Volume I’s source, clock, ﬁeld and record constructions; to the actual
nuclear, atomic and molecular recipients of Volume II; and to Volume III’s exact computation and retained mathematical objects.
Part I establishes ﬁnite admission, particle grammar, mass channels, the nuclear ledger and the retained binding benchmark. Part
II develops the spin source and its complete scientiﬁc response. Part III constructs the actual nuclear states, causal contact histories,
occupied atoms, moving molecules, common-energy isotope tests and carrier/quantum receivers. The spin section provides the
physical and mathematical mechanisms used by these matter recipients. The binary spin coordinates, physical particle inventories
and electronic spin labels retain their own source deﬁnitions.
The numerical results, source hypotheses, controls and classiﬁcations are taken from the identiﬁed completed records. The
chapter organization and connective explanation are editorial synthesis. The underlying source families retain their own Hamiltonian normalizations, preparations, clock conventions, ﬁelds and complete reference energies. The current physical spin head is
the completed H002689 source Dirichlet and six-coordinate receiving calculation. The later layered dense-source solver is an additional completed computational route. Stopped C2 optimization and the partial RAM comparison retain their recorded statuses.
Future mixed Dirichlet blocks, higher action norms and physical calibration inputs remain identiﬁed next operands.
The preceding fourteen-chapter edition is preserved separately. Its material is integrated here with ﬁve new spin chapters,
explicit cross-references and a deduplicated source register. Exact source snapshots, hashes, chapter and coverage maps, build
sources and layout checks accompany the editable edition. Historical receipts preserve their recorded engine versions. Preparing
this manuscript ran no new scientiﬁc calculation, ﬁt or research campaign.

SAM VOLUME II

Matter admission and ﬁnite closure
Chapter I
These equations have different logical roles. Choosing the
structural framework and interpreting its quantities are model
commitments. Once the values and operations are ﬁxed, consequences such as 144/8 = 18 are exact arithmetic. A reported
test may then establish that a particular implementation reproduces its speciﬁed source table or survives selected controls.
A comparison against measured masses adds another layer,
whose units, anchors and statistical scope must be stated. None
of these layers should be silently substituted for another.
The distinction matters even when a formula is short. The
equation R = 12 can be a locked input to a downstream test
rather than an independently measured output of that test. The
equation M = 126 can express retained structural capacity
while the element roster retains its own source and observation status. A row labeled by a familiar particle name can be
the outcome of a reveal stage rather than the object from which
a generator was derived blind. The chapters that follow retain
the order in which the information enters.
This book describes the established construction at those levels. Exact derivations are given with their domains. Computational results are reported as results of the cited records unless
an inspection is speciﬁcally described. Measured comparisons
remain comparisons. The physical interpretation is developed
where the evidence supports it and bounded where the construction has not supplied the necessary bridge.

I.1 The question at the matter boundary
The Substrate Accumulation Model begins with matter as a
source of accumulation. That statement leaves a question on
the source side: what counts as matter, and what kind of closed
object is allowed to supply the source? Volume I develops
the accumulation ﬁeld and its readouts. This volume develops
the ﬁnite objects that enter the matter account, the distinctions
among their source and mass channels, and the ways in which
they return support to the accumulation description.
The central construction is a typed ﬁnite closure. Connector support supplies relations; carrier routing supplies the trafﬁc needed by the conﬁguration; an admission condition distinguishes an eligible closed write from a candidate that does not
close as matter. A numerical value is one ﬁeld of such a construction. It is not a substitute for the construction itself. A
connector and a material row can carry equal numbers while
answering different questions. A carrier can be essential to a
material conﬁguration without becoming another material constituent. A ﬁnite catalog can contain rejected candidates without declaring those candidates to be physical objects.
The matter account therefore has two complementary directions. In the ﬁrst, a supported conﬁguration is admitted as a
ﬁnite material write. In the second, an admitted write emits
a source-support quantity that is decomposed and compressed
before it enters the accumulation readout. The directions meet
at the admitted object, but they are not inverse formulas. In
particular, a source-support number is evaluated after the candidate has passed its declared matter-admission gate.
This distinction is the organizing principle of the volume. It
makes possible a readable account of particle grammar, mass
lift, nuclear ledgers, binding, isotope families and carrier return without treating every occurrence of the same integer as
the same object. It also permits failed physical identiﬁcations
to remain in the record while preserving exact algebra that survives them. The direct source-to-rest-mass bridge is an important example: its failure constrains the interpretation of the
source channel; it does not erase the ﬁnite ledger from which
the channel was constructed.[1, 2]

The interpretation is a complementary split into unlike roles.
The released side is carrier trafﬁc; the retained side is capacity for ﬁnite writes. Nothing in the addition law makes the
carrier an ordinary matter row. Nor does the retained capacity
equation supply all the predicates needed to decide which individual candidates are admitted. It supplies a ﬁnite structural
account within which those decisions are made.
The decomposition also explains why the numbers recur at
different stages. A construction may use 18 as a carrier quantity, a coordinate may evaluate to 18, and a debit may have
magnitude 18. Those occurrences can be related by a speciﬁc
operator or a tested crosswalk, but the shared numeral is not
the crosswalk. This rule becomes especially important when
the matter account reaches nuclear coordinates and informational geometry.

(I.9)

The notation summarizes the source-deﬁned gate; it does not
replace its class-speciﬁc predicates with a new universal formula. The point of the abstraction is to show what information
the gate receives and what category it may emit.
A native generator acts earlier in the chain. It can assign
a number to a candidate that is subsequently rejected by stability or matter admission. This is why a generator identity
can hold for a fake closure. The generator and the rejection
predicate are not inconsistent: they answer different questions.
Likewise, a hidden-support row can carry a nonzero structural
value without producing matter-side source support.
This ordering prevents an otherwise easy circular argument.
Suppose a formula can be evaluated on any number. If a
nonzero output were then taken as sufﬁcient for matter admission, the formula would be both the candidate construction

If the row were counted as material simply because its native value is nonzero, the resulting inventory would lose the
distinction between candidate and admitted object. If it were
treated as a carrier simply because the number is 18, its actual
source role would also be overwritten. The null witness therefore tests a stronger discipline than numerical consistency: the
complete row must be interpreted with its native value, debit,
admission status and output together.

The direct bridge tested in CR239 proposed that one scale
could identify a native source quantity with measured inertial
and gravitational mass:
mi (P) = mg (P) = µQ Q(P).

The reported non-anchor result fails the locked comparison. The direct bridge has RMS residual 0.189480, while the
mass-number control has RMS 0.00202. The source result
records a failed direct bridge. Its isotope increment is also too
small: the proposed increment µQ /8 = 24/7117 u is approximately 0.003372 u, and the reported measured-to-predicted ratios across fourteen isotope pairs have mean 296.45 and median 296.66.[3]
The residual is structured. The source account reports RMS
0.0023 for twelve balanced N = Z rows, compared with 0.2152
for thirty-seven rows with neutron excess, and Spearman correlation +0.984 with N − Z. The native quantity still contains
information: its shufﬂed control is worse. But information in a
source variable is weaker than the claimed direct identiﬁcation
with measured mass.
The correction preserves the coupling channel and separates
the rest-source channel. This is the starting point of the nuclear
and binding chapters. The ﬁnite ledger remains useful because
the failure identiﬁes which quantity was being asked to do too
much. A different ﬁeld is not introduced merely to conceal a
bad residual; the record retains the rejected identity, its anchor,
its control and the neutron-excess structure that motivates the
separation.
I.10 What the ﬁrst closure supplies
Finite matter enters through typed closure. Its admitted support splits into complementary carrier and retained channels,
while operators, hidden support and matter rows keep their distinct roles. The failed direct source-as-mass bridge supplies
the reason to retain separate coupling and rest-source channels. The next chapters develop the ﬁnite particle surfaces and
nuclear ledger; the later source chapters construct their conditional states, contact histories, occupied atoms and molecular
recipients.

SAM VOLUME II

Typed Particle Grammar and Finite Surfaces
Chapter II
II.1 What a particle row contains

II.2 A family of ﬁnite surfaces

The transition from a ﬁnite closure to a particle catalog requires more than a list of integers. A partition value supplies
an address component; it does not by itself say whether the addressed object is a single write, a composite, a carrier, a hidden
support packet, or a rejected closure. The grammar developed
here gives those distinctions an explicit place. Its central object
is a typed row whose identity includes the source table, the row
schema, the address and the operator that acts on that address.
A conventional particle name, when available, is attached later.
This ordering makes the ﬁnite construction recoverable even
where a familiar name is absent.
A useful abstract record is

There is no single unqualiﬁed “number of SAM particles” that
can replace all source counts. The compact mass closure contains 35 rows. Its purpose is to recover the compact prediction chain. The full ﬁnite catalog contains 321 rows and includes candidates, bound objects, carriers, hidden support and
rejected closures. The normalized-lift table contains 139 rows,
of which 138 have a deﬁned quotient. The matter inventory
contains 126 rows, divided into 63 stable single writes and 63
bound composites. The tensor-compatible stable surface contains 80 rows. The periodic construction contains 126 elementfamily rows. Finally, the N100 computation basis contains 100
typed rows after its declared pruning. These surfaces answer
different questions.
For a source catalog C and a source-deﬁned predicate P, one
may write
SP = {r ∈ C : P(r) = 1}.
(II.2)

r = (source, schema, id, class, address,
bin, status, operator, outputs).

(II.1)

The address can contain a partition tuple, sign or charge coordinate, radix depth, route combination and surface ﬁelds. Not
every schema uses every ﬁeld. In particular, a sign ﬁeld attached to a debit operator cannot automatically be substituted
for an electric charge or for a matter–antimatter status. The
output record should likewise keep native value, source support, debit, admission and external comparison distinct. This
is an editorial expression of the source’s typing requirements,
not a claim that one universal row format already implements
all physical sectors.
The adopted structural inputs remain D = 3, αH = 2, R = 12
and S = 8. They enter exact conditional formulas below. The
formulas are exact within their declared domains; the choice
of domain and the physical interpretation of the outputs are
additional statements. Thus the manuscript can preserve a successful algebraic compression without presenting it as an independently demonstrated ontology of all particles.
The principal sources for this chapter are SAMA-D000026,
Typed Matter Surfaces and Row Grammar, and SAMAD000027, Finite Particle Grammar and 321-Row Census, as
assembled in the unapproved review rendering; the current integrated manuscript supplies the surrounding authority map.1
[1, 2, 3] The distinctions in this chapter are necessary for reading the mass formulas in Chapter III and the nuclear constructions that follow.

Other relations between source universes and surfaces are projections rather than simple subset selections. In either case, the
predicate or projection is part of the deﬁnition. Equality of cardinalities does not imply equality of members, and equality of
members would not, without a schema map, imply equality of
interpretation. Two separate surfaces can each have 126 rows
while one organizes admitted writes and the other organizes
element families.
This distinction resolves an otherwise tempting confusion
with the retained capacity M = 126. The arithmetic M = 7R2 /8
is a structural capacity statement. A 126-row inventory is a
census under a particular gate. A 126-row periodic table is a
family-indexed construction. The recurring integer may motivate a proposed structural relation, but the relation has to be
supplied rather than inferred from the repeated numeral. The
same applies to the 80-row surface and the 81-place full face:
one cannot turn an operator into an extra particle merely to
make the totals coincide.
The 139-row lift table provides a particularly useful example. Its source construction comprises 126 promoted rows plus
13 carrier-tensor rows. It is neither a new census of 139 material particles nor an inconsistent version of the 126-row inventory. Its question is which normalized lift operator applies
to each supplied row. One row has both zero source and zero
debit, so the table contains an endpoint that cannot be evaluated as a quotient. Retaining that row is necessary to preserve
the schema; excluding it from quotient arithmetic is necessary
to preserve mathematics.
The older CR119 reveal records 321 particle-catalog rows,
126 matter rows, 126 periodic rows and 573 identityassignment rows. Those numbers already belonged to separate
schemas. CR252 subsequently establishes the current 321-row
reconstruction baseline; it does not erase the older reveal or

II.4 Generation, admission and reveal
II.3 The current 321-row reconstruction

The operational sequence is

CR252 records a deterministic catalog refresh. Its seven bins
have the exact distribution
321 = 63 + 25 + 42 + 169 + 6 + 8 + 8,
= stable + resonance + antimatter
+ bound + carrier + hidden + rejected.

typed address −→ native generator
−→ stability classiﬁcation
−→ matter admission
−→ label or comparator reveal.

(II.3)

(II.4)

The stages cannot be compressed into the assertion that a generated number is an observed particle. A generator can evaluate a
rejected closure. A stable candidate can remain unnamed. An
admitted source can possess a nonzero structural value without
that value being a measured rest mass. A known label attached
downstream does not become a permissible input to an earlier
target-barred construction.
This ordering also explains why rejected objects belong in
the evidence record. If every row that matched a native formula were automatically admitted, the formula would have no
independent stability content. The three-body extension below
supplies a direct counterexample: thirteen rejected source rows
match the same generator as stable candidates. They preserve
the distinction between structural evaluation and the later gate.
Removing them from the presentation would make the successful generator appear stronger than it is.
The identity should remain reversible before an authorized
conventional name is attached. A SAM placeholder can identify the source row without implying that an experimental identiﬁcation exists. When a source does authorize contact with
a conventional name, that contact is retained with its provenance. Neither inventing familiar names nor refusing all familiar names serves the construction; the appropriate practice is
to carry the exact naming authority with the row.

The words in the second expression stand for the corresponding counts in the ﬁrst. In full, there are 63 stable-matter
rows, 25 unstable-resonance rows, 42 antimatter-conjugate
rows, 169 bound-composite rows, six carrier-only rows, eight
hidden-source-support rows and eight rejected-fake-closure
rows. These are the catalog’s organizational bins, not seven
lists of experimentally identiﬁed species.
The inspected CR252 summary reports 321 baseline rows,
321 regenerated rows, stable candidate identiﬁers, complete
classiﬁcation and 321 unchanged rows present in both versions. Baseline, regenerated output and the second-run wrongcontrol receipt share the SHA-256 recorded in the source manifest. The reconstruction retains six carriers with zero promotions. It also records 286 rows satisfying its broader matterallowed predicate. That ﬁeld is not a sum to be guessed from
the English bin labels: it is a separately evaluated predicate in
the 321-row schema. Its value cannot replace either the 126row inventory or the 80-row promoter.
The available runner makes the meaning of this result concrete. It snapshots the existing QP093A catalog, invokes the
QP093A generator, and compares the regenerated output with
the baseline. This is evidence of deterministic reconstruction
of an existing artifact. It is not a second, independent discovery of the catalog or an empirical observation of all its members. The present manuscript uses the inspected receipts without claiming a new execution of the generator.2
[8] The amendment is part of the result. The original V-3/F2
speciﬁcation produced a failure; a precommit amendment corrected the criterion, while preserving that original failure in the
audit trail. A readable account therefore says that the amended
reconstruction passes, rather than silently presenting the ﬁnal
PASS as the only event. The amended test’s useful content is
the unchanged artifact and its correctly typed comparison. The
history of a speciﬁcation correction does not weaken a valid
identity check, but neither should an identity check erase its
own provenance.

decomposition.

II.8 One-body ladders, carriers and hidden support

II.9 How to compare rows without losing the grammar

CR131 assigns the V4_1 single-write family to a fermion-halfwrite class. Its 90-row law is

Three elementary comparisons summarize the practical use of
the grammar. First, compare two differently ordered bound
pairs. Their native values agree because both the product
and the absolute difference are exchange-symmetric. Their
unequal-pair lift can change sign because that operator uses
sgn(a − b). Thus equality in the native column is compatible
with inequality in the signed-debit column. The combination is
not an inconsistency: it records two different questions about
the same ordered address. An implementation that deduplicated rows solely by native value would destroy the information needed for the second question.
Second, compare a stable candidate and a rejected threebody closure. Both can satisfy M3 = 36 ∑ a2i exactly. Their
difference lies in the later stability and admission ﬁelds. A
generator-only export would make those rows look equally admissible, while an admission-only export would conceal the
useful fact that the formula crosses the stability boundary. The
complete record needs both. This is why retaining rejections is
scientiﬁcally productive rather than merely archival: it identiﬁes precisely which operation has, and which operation lacks,
discriminatory power.
Third, compare a hidden support packet with an admitted
charged matter row. Both can contain the factor p(1 + p/144).
In the hidden packet this is a native structural value and the
matter source remains zero. In the compact charged row the
factor appears inside an admitted qA law with a sign-dependent
coefﬁcient and a depth multiplier. An algebra system may legitimately factor the same polynomial in both expressions; a
physical interpretation may not replace one row type with the
other. Symbolic identity of a factor is a relation between formulas, not an admission rule.
These examples suggest a minimal comparison contract. A
comparison speciﬁes the source versions, the row identiﬁers
or reversible address maps, the columns being compared, the
units or native conventions, and the operator domains. An
equality claim then says whether it concerns exact rational values, serialized bytes, class membership or agreement with an
external comparator. Those four senses of agreement are useful but distinct. CR252’s bit identity is stronger than approximate numerical agreement about its serialized artifact, yet it
says nothing by itself about how closely every artifact ﬁeld describes nature. Conversely, an experimental comparison need
not be bit-identical to a structural table to be informative.
The same discipline clariﬁes the term “zero free parameters.”
Once an in-sample formula has been selected and frozen, it can
have no adjustable coefﬁcients when evaluated on that table.
That fact does not erase the earlier opportunity to choose the
formula after seeing the table. A forward-blind seal constrains
future use, but only a subsequently eligible case can supply
the future test. The manuscript therefore preserves the exact
formulas, their ﬁnite match counts and the chronology of selection together. This offers a more useful account than either
dismissing all ﬁnite exactness or presenting every exact match
as a ﬁrst-principles prediction.
The catalog consequently serves two roles at once. It is
a ﬁnite mathematical object whose arithmetic and reconstruc-

III.1 The question a mass formula answers
A formula containing a particle label and a number is not yet a
mass prediction. It may compute a native structural value, an
admitted source contribution, a debit on a surface, a normalized lift, or a downstream readout expressed in physical units.
These quantities can be related, but their relations have to be
stated. The particle grammar supplies the row and selects the
operator; this chapter follows the distinct channels through that
selection. The central lesson of the source record is constructive: retaining the type of a quantity turns several apparently
irregular numerical patterns into exact formulas, while also exposing a direct source-to-mass identiﬁcation that fails.
Write Mnative for the native generator output, qA for admitted
matter-source support, X for a speciﬁed surface debit or credit,
and K for the native source used by a lift-table row. The dimensionless quotient Y = X/K, when deﬁned, is a ﬁfth object.
A measured comparator is another ﬁeld. In a nuclear aggregation the symbols Qsub and Qmass distinguish coupling-source
and rest-source constructions. None of these distinctions can
be removed simply because a particular anchor makes two numerical values coincide.
The source symbol Mobs is likewise a readout ﬁeld whose
meaning must be obtained from its schema. Its subscript alone
is not proof that its value is an experimentally measured mass.
A row’s source provenance and physical conversion map determine whether the number is native, converted, compared or
measured. This matters especially when moving between the
compact base 35-row chain, the 139-row lift table and the 80row tensor-compatible surface. They share some conceptual
machinery but do not share an unqualiﬁed numerical column.
The detailed source accounts are SAMA-D000028, Mass,
Lift, Debit and Particle Readout, and SAMA-D000029, Higgs
and Neutrino Packets. The technical spine preserves the same
channel split and the decisive failure-to-correction sequence.[1,
2, 3] The presentation below derives the arithmetic that can be
checked directly, identiﬁes results reported by existing tests,
and retains the external anchors on which physical-unit comparisons depend. It does not rerun those tests or supply an
unstated universal conversion from structural number to measured mass.

and adding the retained undeﬁned endpoint restores the full table count of 139. These counts, rather than a blanket statement
that all 139 quotients close, express the actual ﬁnite completion.

III.13 What the mass sector hands forward
The mass sector now has several ﬁnite results that can be used
without merging their evidentiary roles. The lift table is complete as a typed table: 139 rows, seven evaluable classes covering 138 quotients, and one explicitly undeﬁned endpoint. The
compact admitted surface has charged and neutral source laws,
together with a non-row conjugation transform and an exact
hard zero. The nuclear branch has a preserved failure of direct source-to-mass conversion and a corrected two-channel
construction whose uniqueness test did not pass. The Higgs
record contains two distinct packets with different operations
and chronology. The neutrino record separates an exact ratio
from its measured scale and retains its non-anchored residual.
These results provide the next chapters with a disciplined
interface. A nuclear ledger may aggregate the corrected channels, but must not identify them merely because balanced matter makes them equal. A binding readout may use the channel
gap, but must supply its own nonlinear operation and energy
conversion. A computation may serialize exact rational operators, but must preserve their class triggers and undeﬁned
endpoints. A physical comparison may open measured targets,
but must keep them separate from the inputs of a target-barred
construction.
The beneﬁt of this separation is not only caution. It is also
economy. One does not need to solve mixing, resolve candidate uniqueness, or launch a new ﬁtting campaign in order to
preserve an exact ﬁnite lift table. Conversely, a well-preserved
ﬁnite table does not solve those other problems by implication. The source record can therefore be complete as a record
of what was constructed and tested, while the larger physical
interpretation remains bounded by the evidence actually available.

SAM VOLUME II

The Nuclear Ledger: Constituents, Channels, and Isotope Structure
Chapter IV
A nucleus brings together two questions that are easy to confuse. What constituents have been counted, and what does
their combination do? The ﬁrst question admits a ﬁnite accounting answer. The second requires a readout of the bound
conﬁguration. This chapter develops the accounting answer
in enough detail that its exact content can be inspected independently of any later energy ﬁt. Chapter V then follows the
additional steps needed to compare nuclear readouts with measurements.
The construction has four slots, six aggregate channels, and
a restricted population domain. Its value is not that six equations replace nuclear dynamics. Its value is that source support,
rest-source bookkeeping, and constituent counts can be carried
together without being silently identiﬁed. This resolves an ambiguity inherited from the particle problem: a source quantity
can participate in matter construction without already being a
measured rest mass. Nuclear aggregation makes that distinction particularly visible because balanced and excess neutrons
have the same constituent-count entries but different couplingsource assignments.
The primary manuscript statement is SAMA-D000030, preserved in Section 7 of the frozen Matter and Finite Closure
manuscript. The technical spine, Sections IV.1–IV.3, supplies the CR247–CR248–CR278 consolidation history and the
failed control discussed below. These are the sources for the
construction. The derivations in this chapter expand their
stated equations; they do not introduce a newly ﬁtted nuclear
model.[1, 2, 3]

electron count. The nucleon populations alone sum to Z + N =
A.
An excess-neutron population cannot be negative. Consequently, substituting a proton-rich isotope into the formulas
may produce a formal algebraic expression, but it does not produce an admissible four-population construction under Equation (IV.2). This matters for the familiar light examples: ordinary hydrogen and helium-3 cannot be used as literal demonstrations of this population basis. Deuterium, tritium, helium4, and neutron-rich nuclei can. A larger treatment may adopt
another basis for the excluded domain, but the present derivation does not supply one by changing a minus sign.
The electron slot makes this a neutral atomic bookkeeping
extension of a nuclear core. It does not locate electrons inside
the nucleus. Nor does the slot’s zero in the nuclear rest-source
column mean that an electron has zero physical rest mass. The
column is deﬁned for the nuclear aggregate being constructed.
The electron count is retained so that charge balance and the
neutral readout convention remain visible; a complete atomic
mass comparison must separately respect electron mass and
electronic binding conventions.
Finally, “balanced” and “excess” designate positions in a
source-accounting basis. The construction does not establish
two experimentally distinguishable neutron species. A basis
may separate contributions that are equal in some observables
and unequal in another channel. Such a separation is useful
precisely when it is kept distinct from a claim about new particles.

IV.6 What equal-population symmetry means
At N = 2Z, the four population entries are all equal:
n p = nnb = nne = ne = Z.

A permutation of equal coefﬁcients leaves their weighted sum
unchanged. In particular, swapping the balanced and excess
neutron vectors between equal populations cannot change the
aggregate. The individual vectors are still different. The invariance belongs to the population-weighted total at this speciﬁc
relation between N and Z.
The technical spine records that CR278 retained a
BOUNDARY artifact verdict because a precommitted wrong
control required a balanced/excess label swap to break every
N > Z row. It broke 55 of 56 such rows. Tritium, with N = 2Z,
was the exception.[2] This outcome can be understood exactly,
without changing the source verdict.
To see the mechanism, let the original neutron contribution be Z ϕnb + (N − Z)ϕne . The swapped contribution is

IV.5 Worked ledger examples
Small examples expose what the general formulas actually
count. They are arithmetic illustrations, not new numerical
experiments or energy predictions.
For deuterium, Z = N = 1, and the population vector is
(1, 1, 0, 1). The constituent counts are u = d = 3 and e = 1.
Both source channels equal 8κ , and the gap is zero. This example shows the cancellation in its smallest neutral admissible
instance. The absence of a gap does not remove the measured
binding of deuterium; rather, it shows why the gap alone cannot be identiﬁed with total binding.
For tritium, Z = 1 and N = 2, giving populations (1, 1, 1, 1).
Its constituent counts are u = 4, d = 5, and e = 1. The two
channels are

that helium-4’s physical energy is twice deuterium’s: that inference would apply linear source accounting to a nonlinear
bound-state observable.
For an iron-56 ledger example, Z = 26 and N = 30. The populations are (26, 26, 4, 26); the constituent counts are u = 82,

mass. Its inputs are the isotope counts and the frozen slot assignments.
These examples also clarify the role of an isotope name. The
label is a convenient way to specify (Z, N) for a known isotope.
The algebra operates on the counts. It does not acquire additional predictive authority because a row happens to carry a
familiar name. Conversely, applying the same algebra to an
unobserved count pair produces a model ledger, not evidence
that the corresponding nucleus can be synthesized or will persist.

source yields (N − Z)2 /A. This carries the two-channel structure into the nonlinear asymmetry readout. Its coefﬁcient retains the adopted source assignments, and physical energy conversion uses its own speciﬁed map.

IV.8 From an isotope row to an element family
An isotope row is indexed by (Z, N), with A determined by
their sum. An element-family row ﬁxes Z while permitting a
collection of neutron counts. A representative isotope is one
chosen member used to display or evaluate that family. These
are three different objects. A table with one row per element
does not become a complete isotope inventory merely because
each row has a neutron count attached.
At ﬁxed Z, changing N traces the source increments described above. The four-slot construction supplies a uniform
way to write those increments, but it does not determine which
isotope should represent the family. A selector for abundance,
longevity, a historical benchmark, or a model frontier would
be additional information. That selector must remain visible
when a binding score is reported over an element table; otherwise, row selection can be mistaken for a universal claim
across all isotopes.
The technical spine distinguishes the historical 162-row
scoped isotope roster from the 126-row native periodic surface
and the one-representative-per-element CR277 binding readout. Those counts answer different questions. They should not
be forced into agreement by adding or deleting rows from the
nuclear ledger. The next chapters use these distinct surfaces
where appropriate, preserving their selectors and limits.
IV.9 The exact handoff to binding
The ledger exports constituent counts, a total rest source, a
coupling source, and a signed imbalance. It does not export
a physical radius, a state vector, a force law, a half-life, or a
general binding energy. The absence of those outputs is not
an algebraic defect. It deﬁnes the boundary between the ﬁnite
construction and the physical readout problem.
A useful handoff is therefore explicit: construct the population vector, accumulate the two source channels, retain
their difference, and only then apply a speciﬁed readout. The
readout must name its geometry, coefﬁcients, unit conversion,
and comparison target. If measured binding were allowed
to choose the native population assignment, construction and
evaluation would no longer be separated. If a linear sum of slot
debits were called the full energy law, the accounting identity
would be asked to do work it was never deﬁned to do.
The next chapter follows an exact consequence of these

SAM VOLUME II

Binding: exact structure, historical ﬁts and the retained held test
Chapter V
Binding is where an exact constituent ledger encounters a
harder question: how does the conﬁguration change the observable mass or energy of the combined system? The ledger
of Chapter IV answers which typed contributions have been assembled. It does not make their energy additive. A nucleus can
share the same total nucleon count with another nucleus while
differing in charge, imbalance, parity, and conﬁguration. A
useful binding readout must respond to those differences without letting the measured answer determine the construction it
is meant to test.
The record contains three distinct achievements. First, the
source-channel gap has an exact normalized-square identity.
Second, historical models use that structure within ﬁtted nuclear readouts and preserve both successful comparisons and
failures. Third, the current B5T authority reports a targetblind informational tensor assignment followed by a leakagecontrolled held-chain residual comparison. These achievements are related, but none should be substituted for another.
The exact identity is not a complete energy formula; the ﬁt is
not a derivation of all its coefﬁcients; and the held comparison
is not a physical identiﬁcation of the tensor.
This chapter follows that sequence. Its sources are the
frozen SAMA binding chapter SAMA-D000032, the technical spine’s historical synthesis, the inspected CR245 runner,
and the binding authority updated on 16 August 2026. That
frozen authority records the retained B5T benchmark. The
later source, ﬁeld, atomic and common-energy constructions
are developed in the added chapters. Older phrases such as
“present applied closure” in the technical spine are read in their
historical setting rather than used to displace B5T.[1, 2, 7, 4]

warning about uniqueness: low holdout residuals can coexist
with multiple admissible coefﬁcient candidates. Its recorded
candidate-artifact failure is not repaired by referring only to its
small residual. The nuclear argument requires neither a unique
mass candidate nor a universal source-to-mass identity. It requires the two typed channels to remain separate long enough
for their difference to be studied.
This is the conceptual starting point of binding in the present
volume. A source packet may be exact in its own units while
still needing a readout law. The statement that an energy comparison is downstream does not render that comparison arbitrary. It speciﬁes which quantities are constructed, which are
calibrated, and which are tested. Keeping those roles separate
makes a failed comparison capable of correcting the readout
without silently rewriting the input ledger.

The preceding mass analysis established the need to separate
native source support from measured mass. The nuclear evidence makes the reason especially concrete. CR239 tried
a direct bridge with one carbon-12 anchor. The retained

Its residual had Spearman correlation +0.984 with neutron excess N − Z.[1, 3]
An anchor ﬁxes a conversion or normalization at the anchor

carbon-12. It indicated that the direct readout was missing
a dependence associated with neutron imbalance. The failed
result survives as a reason for channel separation rather than
being discarded after a later construction performs better.

Then Equation (V.6) becomes Casym AI 2 . At ﬁxed total size,
the normalized-square quantity grows quadratically with imbalance. At ﬁxed imbalance fraction, it grows linearly with
size. This is the useful structural content of the normalization: the same expression distinguishes an extensive contribution from a relative composition variable.
At N = Z, the quantity is zero. If one adds balanced proton–
neutron packets while keeping the absolute excess ﬁxed, the
numerator stays ﬁxed and the denominator increases. If instead one adds excess neutrons at ﬁxed Z, both numerator and
denominator change. These are different paths through the
ledger, so the normalized result is not simply a constant cost
per neutron. The nonlinearity is already present before an energy coefﬁcient is attached.
The square also removes the sign of the gap. That is appropriate for an asymmetry magnitude, but it discards information. The signed gap remains useful when tracing the original
source channels. A downstream calculation that retains only
its square cannot recover which side of a formally extended
balanced line generated it. This does not harm the stated identity; it explains why the squared channel should not replace the
complete ledger in storage or interpretation.

It is linear in its ﬁve coefﬁcients and nonlinear in the isotope
counts. “Linear ﬁt” in coefﬁcient space therefore does not
mean “linear constituent binding” in physical space.
The terms serve different roles. The volume term scales with
total count; the surface term introduces a different size dependence; the charge-dependent term distinguishes nuclei with different proton populations; the asymmetry term penalizes composition imbalance in the chosen sign convention; and the parity term distinguishes even–even, odd–odd, and mixed-parity
rows. These interpretations explain why a single total-source
scalar cannot reproduce every column. They do not establish
the coefﬁcients from the substrate or determine shell-local details absent from this basis.
A simple sum of constituent debits or contact fees lacks
these distinct dependences unless they have already been inserted into the fees. The historical failure of such a sum is
therefore informative. It excludes a particular shortcut from
ledger to energy. It does not imply that contact structure is irrelevant; it says that counting contacts with the tested linear
rule did not close the binding curvature.

In the conventional count basis, its coefﬁcient is then aasym =
λ Casym . Equation (V.5) ﬁxes the conversion between two representations of the same term. It does not ﬁx λ , the physical
energy unit, or the sign convention for the whole observable. If
λ is ﬁtted, the resulting energy coefﬁcient remains ﬁtted even
though the basis conversion is exact. This is the point at which

V.5 Reading the CR245 implementation literally
The inspected CR245 runner separates several stages that are
sometimes compressed into the phrase “binding from typed
substrate.” Stage one uses exact rational arithmetic to construct the channels and verify Equation (V.6). Stage two ﬁts
the ﬁve-column form to training rows with A ≥ 16. Stage three
searches a ﬁnite collection of typed-rational candidates near
the ﬁtted coefﬁcients. Stage four builds and evaluates the candidate described by the program as “zero-free.” The order matters: the candidate choice occurs after a ﬁt to measured data.[7]
The code also identiﬁes the numerical comparison target.

V.6 What the historical controls establish
A control earns its meaning from the change it actually makes.
In CR245, dropping the asymmetry column and reﬁtting the
remaining four columns asks whether that column adds useful structure in the selected training lane. Shufﬂing the target
labels and reﬁtting asks whether the original association between rows and values carries information. Neither control, by
itself, identiﬁes a microscopic mechanism. Their value lies in
excluding speciﬁc explanations of the ﬁt.
The runner also perturbs an R-dependent factor on the righthand side of the asymmetry identity while holding the constructed source channels ﬁxed. The control replaces the denominator 192 · 7117 = 16 · 12 · 7117 by 16R0 · 7117 for selected alternative R0 . For a nonzero gap, the equality should
then break. This is a check on the frozen identity factor. It is
narrower than regenerating an alternative physical theory with
every R-dependent input changed consistently.
Balanced rows have N = Z, so both sides are zero for any
such factor. The code explicitly separates those trivial rows
from the nontrivial ones. This is the same logical discipline
illustrated by the equal-population exception in Chapter IV: a
control cannot distinguish a change on rows where the measured expression is invariant under that change. Recording the
invariant subset is part of interpreting the test, not a reason to
discard the entire control.
Together these controls establish bounded facts about the
chosen expression and ﬁt. The exact rational stage checks internal equality; term removal checks usefulness within a basis;
shufﬂing checks row-target association; and coefﬁcient perturbation checks dependence on a frozen factor. Combining them
improves the evidence record, but does not collapse their different meanings into a universal proof of nuclear binding.

uses a different reference state. This chapter therefore calls

This distinction is not cosmetic. Changing the reference
mass changes the target and potentially the ﬁtted coefﬁcients.
A familiar-looking basis does not establish that two programs
ﬁtted the same observable. Historical reports using “binding”
must be read together with their actual target deﬁnition. No
new numerical conversion or reﬁt is performed here, and the
stated proxy should not be silently corrected in prose while
leaving the reported metrics untouched.
The coefﬁcient search draws its candidates from a speciﬁed
collection of inherited atoms, reciprocals, ratios, and special
combinations. A ﬁnite deterministic candidate list is a reproducibility aid. However, selecting the candidate closest to an
empirical ﬁt is still data-informed selection. The inspected
function build_zero_free_coef replaces a ﬁtted coefﬁcient if the nearest typed candidate is within ﬁve percent; otherwise it retains the ﬁtted value. Thus the label “zero-free”
does not describe a universally parameter-free, preregistered
prediction.

CR250 obtained a monotonic binding shape, but its designated wrong control did not separate the candidate. Monotonicity alone is weak evidence when many simple functions

asymmetry lane and distinguished the 1/(SL ) scale from the
nearby 1/(SR2 ) scale in its principal lane, while preserving

This history supplies a sequence of increasingly speciﬁc
questions. Can one source scalar serve as measured mass?
Can a contact sum reproduce curvature? Can an alternative
construction separate from its control? Can a coefﬁcient be
isolated from correlated terms? The answers progressively
narrow what the successful readout must contain. They do
not make the unﬁnished portions mandatory work before the
record can be written coherently. The ﬁnished account consists of the derivations, tests, failures, and boundaries already

V.9 The CR277 zero-reﬁt extension
CR277 applied the frozen CR274 base-plus-four-operator
model to a canonical 126-element surface with one representative isotope per element. It reports reproducing the earlier
model predictions at the benchmark anchors to better than
10−3 MeV, then extending the readout without reﬁtting at each
element. Table V.2 records the observed-label comparisons.[2]
The larger error in the light subset is part of the result. Those
rows lie outside the original base-ﬁt range and expose a transfer limitation that would be hidden by quoting only the earlier 55-row score. The 78 extended-only rows similarly isolate
rows absent from the original ﬁtted benchmark. Their RMS
is a more relevant description of that particular extension than
the training-range improvement.
“Zero reﬁt” has a precise and useful meaning here: an already ﬁtted model was applied without another ﬁt to the extension rows. It does not mean that the model had never been
ﬁtted. The distinction is parallel to the CR245 terminology
problem, but the operations are different. CR245 selects candidates near ﬁtted coefﬁcients and may retain those coefﬁcients;
CR277 freezes an existing ﬁtted readout and tests its transfer.
Both can produce meaningful evidence when their histories remain visible.
The eight frontier rows at Z = 119 through 126 receive
sealed model outputs, not observed residuals. Their existence
in the ﬁnite periodic surface cannot be used to enlarge the measured comparison from 118 to 126 observed elements. Nor
does one representative per element establish a full isotopefamily law. The extension is a preserved historical result, superseded as current binding authority by B5T but not erased by
it.

V.8 The gated CR274 readout
CR274 is the later historical applied model in the technical
spine. It starts from a ﬁtted base K, retains the exact asymmetry structure, and adds four family-gated operators. Table V.1
gives the recorded coefﬁcients without promoting them to substrate primitives.[2]
On the 55-row benchmark, the base model reports RMS
3.9102 MeV and the gated model 2.7160 MeV. Fifty of 55
rows lie within 5 MeV. The named anchors oxygen-16, iron-56,
gold-197, and lead-208 lie within 10 MeV, while ﬁve benchmark residual outliers remain. These statements characterize
the reported benchmark. They do not establish performance
on every isotope belonging to the same element families.
A gated model can improve a readout by allowing a shared
correction on rows satisfying a predicate. The scientiﬁc burden then moves partly to the predicate: was it deﬁned independently, was it selected after seeing residuals, and does it
retain performance outside the ﬁtted rows? The coefﬁcients remain empirical whether the predicate has a suggestive geometric name or not. Their proper place is downstream of source
construction, where they can be compared and replaced without modifying the exact ledger.
The source’s phrase that the asymmetry coefﬁcient is locked

then its identity is frozen before measured binding is opened.

V.11 The leakage-safe held-chain comparison
For the measured comparison, the authority reports rebuilding
the exact B4 coordinate lane with out-of-fold predictions for
1,982 development rows and the unchanged 393 held isotopes
across 17 element chains. Out-of-fold construction matters because a downstream residual model should not receive deceptively accurate in-sample predictions from an upstream component. The reported held-chain split further deﬁnes the particular transfer question being tested. It does not turn the held set
into an unlimited statement about all nuclear regimes.
Table V.3 preserves every listed lane. The tensor principal
has the lowest reported RMSE among them. Its improvement
over the direct-3D comparison is about 0.008 MeV in this metric, and over the external-coordinate baseline about 0.027 MeV.
The numerical differences are useful precisely at that scale;
they need not be described as a qualitative solution of binding
to be meaningful.
The count within 1 MeV answers a different question from
RMSE. RMSE squares each residual and therefore responds
strongly to larger errors; the threshold count treats all rows
inside the chosen interval alike. The inconsistent-channel relabel control also has 348 rows within 1 MeV, yet a higher
RMSE. Thus the principal’s advantage is not that it uniquely
maximizes every possible score. It wins the stated RMSE comparisons while sharing one threshold count. Preserving both

V.12 The direction-erased diagnostic and the current
boundary
The B5T authority also retains an important unfavorable comparison for the selected directed construction. A directionerased diagnostic reaches relative RMSE 0.931302002638, below the directed locator’s 0.990887476018. This diagnostic
was not a frozen T threshold and therefore does not change the
reported classiﬁcation. It nevertheless limits how the selected
directional representation should be interpreted.
A diagnostic can expose structure that the formal pass conditions did not require the test to optimize. Here it indicates
that endpoint sign and role/tensor identity may not be most effectively represented by the selected joint construction. That
observation is an existing result, not a license to replace the
frozen principal after seeing the outcome. The source recommends a future target-blind factorization route, while explicitly
saying it is not yet mapped, owner-conﬁrmed, or authorized for
execution. This volume records that boundary without turning
it into a new campaign.
B5T installs an informational three-dimensional tensor assignment. The current authority explicitly does not install a
physical tensor, nuclear geometry, isotope-local tensor, A/qA
27

SAM Volume II | Chapter V

Brady | 19 October 2026

conversion, SI-energy law, Higgs law, or physical W9P crosswalk. It is not B6 and not a ﬁnal binding formula. These limits
describe which bridge remains absent: numerical and representational usefulness has not yet been converted into a physical
account of where nuclear energy resides or how the conﬁguration produces it.
The chapter can therefore end at a completed result rather
than an unﬁnished obligation. The exact source identity is established conditionally. Historical ﬁt and transfer results are
preserved with their empirical inputs. B5T supplies the retained representation and held-residual benchmark. None requires the author to begin another search in order to state accurately what has already been done.
V.13 A binding claim with its inputs still visible
The strongest coherent statement supported by this chain has
several parts. The four-slot source construction gives a signed
neutron-excess gap. Its normalized square yields the asymmetry shape with an exact rational factor. A full readout requires
additional size, charge, parity, and family dependencies; historical attempts demonstrate that linear contact accounting and
direct source-to-mass conversion do not sufﬁce. Fitted gates
improve a stated benchmark but lose accuracy on some zeroreﬁt extensions. The current B5T representation improves the
reported held-chain residual metric relative to its listed comparisons while leaving physical interpretation open.
These are compatible ﬁndings, not successive claims that
earlier limitations have vanished. The same exact algebra
can support several empirical readouts. A better residual
model can coexist with an incomplete physical mechanism. A
failed historical shortcut can remain valuable after the current
method changes. The manuscript’s task is to carry those distinctions intact across the transition from mathematical construction to measurement.
The dedicated spin section next develops exact interacting
sources, thermal preparation, hidden memory and physical receiving response. The nuclear chapters that follow receive
those methods through the actual isotope action and ordered
contact history. Element families, representative isotopes, and
composites can be organized using the ﬁnite framework while
retaining separate questions about stability, binding, and chemistry. That separation permits the volume to be complete as an
account of its results while carrying the remaining physical assignments explicitly.

derived temperature corrections suppress the complete count
response further. At sixth order a new hidden interaction survives every shared thermal correction. Its rational dependence

at the source-to-receiver interface while retaining their different Hamiltonians and clocks.[73][74]

SAM. Volume I receives an explicit ﬁnite-source time convention, a normalized response and source-metric memory,

carrying an occupied source through a changed physical receiver: retain signed mixed functions, full ﬁnite inventory,

source functions, exact matrix contracts, retained histories and
dependency-bound results. The common count ﬁfty-four in
the separate electron sweep supplies no row correspondence
with these spin preparations; actual source maps establish any
physical join.
The completed progression is consequently a detailed physical source/receiver account: original Gibbs ﬂips determine moments, moments determine hidden direction and action, actual
source action tightens upper response, and a newly paid edge
observable raises the lower response. Its physical ﬁeld, background, norm and clock remain identiﬁable at each stage, so
the resulting information can be received and reused across
volumes with its original meaning.

SAM VOLUME II

Elements, isotopes and composite matter
Chapter XIII
The selector supplies one isotope for a one-row-per-element
comparison. It does not invert the family projection on the entire isotope roster. Nor does it assert that the chosen isotope is
the only allowed, stable or physically important member of the
family. Those statements would require additional predicates.
The distinction can be followed directly in a readout. First
construct EZ . Then record the selector that supplies Nrep (Z).
Next evaluate the nuclear or binding operator on the selected
(Z, N) pair. Finally compare the resulting ﬁeld with a measured
value if the row has an authorized comparator. If the selected
isotope changes, the family coordinate may remain ﬁxed while
the readout changes. The selector is therefore part of the comparison speciﬁcation.
This separation also explains why a periodic roster and an
isotope vault can have different sizes without disagreement.
The LC05 account retains 126 native family rows and an older
scoped roster of 162 isotope rows. Neither number describes
the cardinality of every possible isotope family. The ﬁrst is the
declared range of the periodic construction. The second is a
particular retained source roster. Their coexistence is expected
once the row types are stated.

XIII.1 From a nuclear address to a periodic family
The nuclear ledger distinguishes proton number, neutron number and the channels carried by their populations. A periodic
construction asks a different question: how should the ﬁnite
matter account organize families whose members share a proton number? The move from an isotope to an element family
removes information for a particular purpose. It does not turn
every isotope of the family into the same nuclear state.
The current source construction assigns one native family
row to each integer Z from 1 through 126. It audits the native
coordinate before applying a familiar name or measured comparator. The order is important because an externally supplied
label cannot count as a prediction of a construction that used
the label to choose its own input. In this account the native row
is constructed ﬁrst; the label is a downstream ﬁeld.[1, 3]
The ﬁnite periodic surface is useful as an organized model
inventory. It places known-label families and source-native
frontier families within one declared indexing scheme. Its
value does not depend on calling every row an observed element. The scheme can be exact as an inventory while the physical status of an individual frontier row remains unestablished.
The distinction is carried explicitly through this chapter.
Chemistry belongs to this volume because elements, isotopes and composites continue the matter construction. This
placement does not imply that a complete molecular chemistry
operator has been derived. This chapter supplies the family indexing, nuclear populations and source-support channels. The
later chapters then develop the completed occupied atomic and
molecular source constructions, including full pair ﬁelds, collective recoil and paid motion. The family inventory and the
dynamical calculation retain their own deﬁnitions.

XIII.3 Native construction before label reveal
For each permitted coordinate the source emits a row of the
form
EZ = (Z, native_idZ , frontierZ , . . .).
(XIII.4)
The ellipsis is not permission to ﬁll absent physical columns
by analogy. It represents only ﬁelds already supplied by the
source construction. In particular, a native identiﬁer does not
supply a shell conﬁguration, a half-life, a decay channel or a
molecular interaction law.
The periodic invariant audit checks that the coordinate lies
in the declared range, that the number of rows is 126, and that
the coordinates are unique. After that audit the label stage supplies 118 known labels for Z ≤ 118 and eight native frontier
identiﬁers for 119 ≤ Z ≤ 126. These are descriptions of the
source table’s reveal policy. A successful label join validates
the correspondence it actually checks; it does not by itself
establish every physical property associated with the conventional name.
The chronological order prevents label-ﬁrst backﬁlling. In
a label-ﬁrst procedure, a familiar name could select an isotope
and the isotope could then be used to construct the row claimed
to explain that name. That would make a visually convincing table without preserving the direction of inference. The retained procedure puts the invariant audit before reveal so that
the native inventory can be inspected without relying on the
display labels.
This does not make the full construction independent of all
historical discovery. A method can be native-ﬁrst in its exe-

(XIII.5)
native identity and external observation. Their purpose is to
make clear what has been preserved by the replay.
A numerical tolerance in a decimal implementation is compatible with exact underlying rational identities. It records
the implementation’s arithmetic behavior. It does not enlarge
the physical meaning of the identities. A very small splitreconstruction error is evidence about the split calculation, not
evidence that a frontier nucleus has been synthesized or that its
chemical behavior follows from the same tolerance.
The appropriate result is therefore a ﬁnite periodic and isotope record with reproducible labels and source custody. The
completed replay establishes this inventory. The occupied electronic and molecular constructions developed later in this edition provide the subsequent dynamical recipients.

(XIII.6)

These decompositions make clear why equal cardinality cannot be used as an elementwise identity. The membership predicates differ, and the rows carry different kinds of addresses.
A row-position pairing between the two tables would certainly be possible as a display operation: ﬁrst row with ﬁrst
row, second with second, and so on. Such a pairing is not a
source-derived physical relation. To give it scientiﬁc meaning,
the pairing would need to preserve a declared property or follow an independently speciﬁed map. The mere existence of a
bijection between ﬁnite sets of equal size is insufﬁcient.
The source account calls their common value a structural
contact at the retained horizon. That statement can be retained
without overextending it. The construction organizes both surfaces using a common ﬁnite capacity while allowing them to
answer different questions. Keeping two schemas is not an admission of numerical inconsistency. It is what permits their
respective information to remain available.
The same reasoning applies to other totals in the volume.
The 321-row catalog, its 169 bound entries, the 80-row stable
structural surface, and the 100-row computation grammar are
not rival answers to one census question. They are different
selected views. A periodic-family reader need not regard every carrier or rejected catalog candidate as an element, and an
executable grammar need not contain a separate operation for
every isotope represented in an input table.

against a 10−90 tolerance. These are recorded replay quantities,
not a new execution undertaken for this manuscript.[3]

dependence on the locked structural inputs; replacing a frontier
status with a known label challenges the distinction between

XIII.8 What makes a composite more than a collection
A collection of admitted constituents is the beginning of a composite description, not its completion. A composite also requires a connector pattern, a debit or credit operator, a closure
gate and a source-emission rule. The source abstraction is

C = {ri }, Γ, D, Gclose , Esource .
(XIII.12)
The constituent set records which rows are present. The connector pattern Γ records how they are related. The operator D
speciﬁes the relevant debit or credit. The gate decides whether
that connected conﬁguration closes. The emission rule states
how an admitted composite returns support.
Adding catalog indices cannot recover these ﬁelds. Indices
are identiﬁers, and changing the order of a table can change an
index without changing the physical or grammatical object it
identiﬁes. Even if an index happened to equal a useful scalar,
the sum of indices would still omit orientation, pair class, gate
state and emitted support. The composite construction must
preserve the relationships that make the collection one object.
This explains the different composite counts encountered
earlier. CR252 retains 169 bound entries inside the 321-row
catalog. LC06 retains 63 bound writes inside a 126-row matter inventory. A four-slot nucleus is yet another representation.
The entries are selected and grouped under different predicates.
A complete account can preserve all three without manufacturing a universal row-number correspondence.
The four-slot ledger supplies constituent population and
channel information. It does not, by itself, specify every connector of a three-dimensional object. A graph-based reception
construction may add exact incidence for a particular object,
while an empirical binding operator supplies a separate energy
readout. The fact that these descriptions can be joined at selected interfaces does not make each description independently
sufﬁcient for all the others.

XIII.7 Historical readout and zero-reﬁt extension
The historical nuclear readout enters after the nuclear ledger
and asymmetry columns have been constructed. CR274 reports a 55-row ﬁtted benchmark. The base readout has RMS
3.9102 MeV. Four ﬁtted family gates reduce the reported RMS
to 2.7160 MeV, with 50 of 55 rows within 5 MeV. The ﬁtted
gates are parameters of that empirical construction. They do
not become substrate primitives merely because the resulting
expression uses structural coordinates.[3]
CR277 then freezes that operator and extends it to one representative isotope per Z. It ﬁrst reproduces the retained benchmark predictions to less than 10−3 MeV and then evaluates
the extended table without reﬁtting. The sequence is important: construction and ﬁtting precede freezing; benchmark reproduction checks the implemented frozen operator; extension
examines its behavior on a broader representative surface.
The reported observed-label subset contains 118 rows and
has RMS 6.672 MeV. Its light subset, Z = 1 through 7, has
seven rows and RMS 19.907 MeV. The middle subset, Z =
8 through 82, has 75 rows and RMS 4.145 MeV. The heavy
subset, Z = 83 through 118, has 36 rows and RMS 5.752 MeV.
The extended-only comparison has 78 rows and RMS 7.974
MeV.
These ﬁgures should be read together. The full-table residual is not represented adequately by the more favorable middle subset, and the light-row miss should not disappear when
a global value is quoted. The zero-reﬁt extension provides information precisely because the operator was not adjusted to
absorb every new deviation. It reveals a domain in which the
frozen readout transfers less well.
Zero reﬁt is a statement about the extension, not the origin
of the coefﬁcients. It does not convert the historical ﬁtted operator into a wholly parameter-free law. Nor does it turn a
representative-isotope evaluation into a test over all isotopes
of all element families. The frozen readout, the representative
selector, the comparator source and the reported residual must
remain joined in the account.
This historical chain is also distinct from the later current
B5T binding authority discussed in the preceding chapter. The
tests have different representations, data splits and questions.
Their RMS values should not be ranked as though they were
interchangeable scores from one benchmark. The historical
periodic readout remains evidence about that construction’s
extension; B5T remains a later representation and held-chain

XIII.9 From periodic families to occupied and moving matter
The periodic construction supplies family coordinates, labels,
neutral electron counts and isotope grouping. The complete
nuclear source adds conditional states and histories. The next
step receives that source in an occupied electronic system.
The following chapters develop the completed scalar and
polarization Hamiltonian, Pauli occupation, direct and exchange pairs, whole ion/free references and collective recoil.
Their molecular continuation retains all 91 carbon and 153
oxygen moving reference channels, source-sector kinetic motion and paid virtual binding witnesses. These dynamical
constructions receive the inventory and supply the additional
state and interaction data. Reaction rates, resolved molecular spectra and environmental response retain their separate
observables.[12][27]

XVI.1 The question after a complete construction
The completed constructions now supply more than a roster of
isotope labels. They retain legal internal states, occupied electronic sectors, ﬁeld squares, counterterms, recoil, and the free
systems against which formation is measured. This makes a
more speciﬁc physical question possible: can one source law
calculate the bound object and its free constituents in the same
energy convention, and then carry that law across isotopes?
The question connects the exact matter construction to the measured energy comparison. Its operands are the full systems developed in the preceding chapters.
Three recent records make the distinction concrete. The carbon isotope calculation transfers an already speciﬁed source
into a complete prepared-electron family. The light-isotope
calculation tests a particular scalar species-current law against
a held helium extension. The 37-isotope preparation lays out
a common prediction convention and preserves the structural
operands for the next source derivation. These records have
different completed outputs. Their relationship gives the volume a precise source-to-energy map.[65][66][67]
The organization in this chapter is an editorial synthesis of
those completed records. The source hypotheses and numerical outcomes retain their original attribution. Sean Brady is
the originator and conceptual director; OpenAI ChatGPT and
Codex are AI research collaborators and co-authors. In particular, the scalar species-current extension is recorded as a
Codex contribution, rather than attributed to the original matter ledger.

corresponding bounds. It cannot change the reference system
merely by changing the label on a table column. Fragment separation energies likewise come from differences of calculated
endpoints. The 37-isotope preparation explicitly excludes measured light-fragment bindings as ﬁxed offsets in the prediction

The same principle applies to numerical enclosures. Subtracting two variational upper witnesses does not establish an

recipients, as well as the reference-dependent electronic and

SAM VOLUME II

Carrier return and matter reception
Chapter XVII
concerns the sum of two support channels. It says nothing
by itself about which downstream physical quantity should be
identiﬁed with that sum. A construction can preserve its source
quantity exactly while an attempted physical interpretation of
the quantity fails.
The LC03 replay follows the longer matter-to-accumulation
route and retains ﬁfteen rejected wrong controls. The alternatives include missing carrier support, altered fractions, promotion of carriers to matter, vector/scalar substitution, massonly source, omitted compression and direct use of support as
measured mass. Each alternative changes a particular interface
rather than merely perturbing an arbitrary output.
Some of these distinctions can be seen without a numerical
test. Deleting T while retaining W leaves only 7qA /8, so the
original support is no longer conserved. Other alternatives require the source’s full typed structure. If both fractions were
changed so that their sum remained one, the generic arithmetic
conservation equation would still hold, but the speciﬁed oneeighth carrier relation to the structural domain would no longer
be preserved. Conservation alone therefore does not select the
source’s fractions; the locked structural relation and its scoped
controls are also part of the construction.
The tests should be read at this resolution. A rejected wrong
fraction supports dependence on the speciﬁed domain relation.
Its result has the scope of the speciﬁed carrier-transport mechanism. A successful replay establishes the mechanism it actually reconstructs, using the inputs and gates it records.

XVII.1 How a closed object remains a source
Finite closure does not isolate a material object from the substrate account. An admitted row continues to emit source support, and a composite must retain a rule for the support emitted
by its connected conﬁguration. The return route begins with
the admitted object and ends with the source accepted by the
accumulation ﬁeld. It preserves the object as matter throughout the route.

The order matters. Directly substituting qA for measured
mass would omit the carrier and compression stages. Calling

count a channel of support as an added object. Both shortcuts change the source semantics even when they produce a
familiar-looking scalar expression.
The present chapter follows this route through functional
carrier and container roles, then examines the more detailed
ATOM3D reception constructions. The early part concerns
exact ledger distinctions and scoped catalog tests. The later
part concerns ﬁnite information receipts, graph reconstruction
and candidate support demand. The two parts are related, but
neither supplies an automatic conversion from informational
structure to physical length or energy.

XVII.6 From ledgers to an ATOM3D scaffold

The output is a consequence of the transform. The transform
itself is not another particle-bearing row.
CR282 traces a historical A-ﬁeld-carrier row that was retired
as a footprint duplicate. Restoring that row would change the

inappropriate inventory representation of it.[3]
A separate appeal source supports a route from contact

The custody distinction is scientiﬁcally important. Two documents can discuss related concepts while testing different
propositions. A shared numerical identiﬁer or a similar title
is insufﬁcient to transfer a result between them. The present
manuscript retains the available route and the missing connection without creating a new causal edge between matter, activator and carrier.

XVII.9 The accumulation carrier and graph reconstruction
AFC1 selects the separate A-ﬁeld carrier for the local accumulation relay in sixteen of sixteen cases. This carrier is not
the non-row conjugation operator A. Maintaining the distinction prevents the accumulation ﬁeld, an operator on charged
antecedents and a transport role from being collapsed under
the same letter.
AFC2 reconstructs a 3p + 3n object from eighteen targetbarred records. Its degree sequence is 4, 4, 3, 3, 2, 2, and its aggregate quark coordinates are u = d = 9. Wrong-information
controls can preserve the aggregate nine while losing the object. The result therefore tests more than a total: it relies on
relation identities, endpoints and the information needed to reconstruct the graph.
AFC3 applies classical multidimensional scaling to the
anonymous graph. The reported output has rank three, nonzero
tetrahedral volume and four symmetries. The reveal identiﬁes
one full matter exchange, a proper 180-degree rotation about
a common axis containing the three paired midpoints. Scrambled or disconnected ledgers fail the corresponding construction, while tampering and density-only alternatives change the
shape.[5]
The coordinates are unscaled. Rank three establishes the dimension of the reported geometric representation under the selected construction, not a calibrated nuclear size. A proper rotation in that representation is a mathematical symmetry of the
reconstructed object. It does not by itself identify a measured
motion, a binding potential or a dynamical law. The source explicitly leaves physical length, qA , binding and energy outside
this result.
The distinction between exact incidence and displayed coordinates is useful. Incidence identiﬁes which relations exist. A
geometric embedding represents those relations using coordinates under a speciﬁed method. Physical calibration would
be an additional map from that representation to measured
units and observables. Preserving the three stages makes the
ﬁnite reconstruction interpretable without overstating its physical reach.

XVII.12 The return that can be carried forward
The ﬁnite return account is now explicit. An admitted material conﬁguration emits typed support. Its carrier and retained channels conserve that support. Ledger compression
supplies the interface to accumulation. Functional addresses
distinguish output, ﬁxed-point and terminal roles. Selected reception tests reconstruct exact graphs, unscaled geometric representations and relative support behavior from ﬁnite receipts.
The account preserves the failures that deﬁne its reach: no
direct support-as-mass identity, no carrier promotion through a
numerical lift, no duplicate operator row, no successful CR283
row-to-face geometry, no universal calibrated energy transfer,
and no automatic physical interpretation of informational tensors. These limits do not remove the exact constructions. They
identify the information that may be exported without alteration to the computation volume.

SAM VOLUME II

The ﬁnite handoff to computation
Chapter XIX
not a replacement for the admission predicate. A rejected candidate can be represented exactly and can remain valuable as a
control. A hidden-support row can be needed for the grammar
while being barred from matter-source emission. The receiving language should be able to carry those statuses without
turning every stored record into an admitted physical object.
This is not a new implementation speciﬁcation. It states the
semantic content of the source objects already used in the volume. Different implementations may encode the ﬁelds differently, but they cannot omit a load-bearing distinction and still
claim to reproduce the same scientiﬁc object.

XIX.1 What leaves the matter volume
The matter volume ends with objects that a computation can
receive without changing their scientiﬁc status. They include
typed particle rows, native generator forms, admission and stability ﬁelds, mass-lift channels, nuclear population ledgers, element and isotope addresses, carrier budgets, closure addresses
and selected relation receipts. Some are exact algebraic constructions; some are catalog records; some carry measured
comparators or ﬁtted parameters. A faithful computation preserves those distinctions.
The receiving volume develops the Substrate Ledger Computer and its language, exact-state representations, write lifecycle and applications. It does not need to repeat every physical
argument in order to serialize a matter row. It does need to
know what the row means, which operations are deﬁned on it
and which results remain only conditional or empirical. The
handoff is therefore a contract between types and operators,
rather than a claim that execution supplies missing physical
evidence.[1, 2]
The conceptual route is ﬁnite matter grammar, typed mass
and binding channels, carrier/container return, computation
grammar, language and write lifecycle. This route connects
the volumes while leaving ownership of each result clear. Matter deﬁnitions and comparisons remain in Volume II. Runtime
behavior and computational exploitation belong to Volume III.
A successful execution may validate its implementation of the
received rule; it does not by itself validate the rule as a description of nature.

XIX.3 Exact rationals before decimal display
Several central quantities are rational:
